Configure dependency review as a required workflow in your repository rulesets to ensure consistent enforcement.</p><p>Example dependency review workflow:</p><div class="hextra-code-block hx-relative hx-mt-6 first:hx-mt-0 hx-group/code"><div><div class=highlight><pre tabindex=0 class=chroma><code class=language-yaml data-lang=yaml><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=nt>name</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>Dependency Review</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w></span><span class=nt>on</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=p>[</span><span class=l>pull_request]</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w></span><span class=nt>permissions</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>  </span><span class=nt>contents</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>read</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>  </span><span class=nt>pull-requests</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>write</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w></span><span class=nt>jobs</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>  </span><span class=nt>dependency-review</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>    </span><span class=nt>runs-on</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>ubuntu-latest</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>    </span><span class=nt>steps</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>      </span>- <span class=nt>name</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>Checkout code</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>        </span><span class=nt>uses</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>actions/checkout@v5</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>      </span>- <span class=nt>name</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>Dependency Review</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>        </span><span class=nt>uses</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>actions/dependency-review-action@v4</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>        </span><span class=nt>with</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>          </span><span class=c># Block pull requests with vulnerabilities at moderate severity or higher</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>          </span><span class=nt>fail-on-severity</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>moderate</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>          </span><span class=c># Block dependencies with incompatible licenses</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>          </span><span class=nt>deny-licenses</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>GPL-2.0, GPL-3.0, LGPL-2.0, LGPL-3.0, AGPL-3.0</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>          </span><span class=c># Post detailed summary in PR comments</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>          </span><span class=nt>comment-summary-in-pr</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>always</span></span></span></code></pre></div></div><div class="hextra-code-copy-btn-container hx-opacity-0 hx-transition group-hover/code:hx-opacity-100 hx-flex hx-gap-1 hx-absolute hx-m-[11px] hx-right-0 hx-top-0"><button class="hextra-code-copy-btn hx-group/copybtn hx-transition-all active:hx-opacity-50 hx-bg-primary-700/5 hx-border hx-border-black/5 hx-text-gray-600 hover:hx-text-gray-900 hx-rounded-md hx-p-1.5 dark:hx-bg-primary-300/10 dark:hx-border-white/10 dark:hx-text-gray-400 dark:hover:hx-text-gray-50" title="Copy code"><div class="copy-icon group-[.copied]/copybtn:hx-hidden hx-pointer-events-none hx-h-4 hx-w-4"></div><div class="success-icon hx-hidden group-[.copied]/copybtn:hx-block hx-pointer-events-none hx-h-4 hx-w-4"></div></button></div></div></li><li><p><strong>Secret scanning push protection</strong>: Always aim to enable secret scanning push protection organization-wide. When first establishing new secret scanning custom patterns, allow bypass to soft-block developers while you analyze the frequency and use cases for false positives and refine your patterns. Once patterns are validated, consider removing bypass permissions for critical repositories. Push protection prevents secrets from ever entering the repository history, which is more secure than detection after commit.</p></li></ol><h4>PW.6: Configure the compilation, interpreter, and build processes to improve executable security<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=pw6-configure-the-compilation-interpreter-and-build-processes-to-improve-executable-security></span>
<a href=#pw6-configure-the-compilation-interpreter-and-build-processes-to-improve-executable-security class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h4><p><strong>Summary</strong>: Ensure build processes use secure tools and configurations in highly controlled environments.</p><p><strong>GitHub implementation scope</strong>: <em>GitHub-native</em></p><p><strong>Implementation details</strong>:</p><p>Use GitHub-hosted runners to provide secure, ephemeral build environments with up-to-date software.</p><ol><li><p><strong>GitHub-hosted runners</strong>: Use GitHub-hosted runners for all CI/CD workflows to leverage:</p><ul><li>Automatically updated operating systems and build tools</li><li>Ephemeral environments that are destroyed after each job</li><li>Isolation between builds preventing cross-contamination</li><li>Consistent, hardened base images maintained by GitHub</li></ul></li><li><p><strong>Runner image selection</strong>: Choose appropriate runner images based on your build requirements:</p><ul><li><code>ubuntu-latest</code>, <code>windows-latest</code>, <code>macos-latest</code> for standard builds</li><li>Larger runner sizes (<code>ubuntu-latest-4-core</code>, <code>ubuntu-latest-8-core</code>) for resource-intensive builds</li><li>Runner images are updated weekly with security patches and toolchain updates</li></ul></li><li><p><strong>Workflow security configurations</strong>:</p><ul><li>Use pinned action versions with commit SHAs for supply chain security</li><li>Minimize workflow permissions using the <code>permissions</code> key</li><li>Avoid using self-hosted runners unless absolutely necessary for specialized hardware or network requirements</li></ul></li></ol><div class="hx-overflow-x-auto hx-mt-6 hx-flex hx-rounded-lg hx-border hx-py-2 ltr:hx-pr-4 rtl:hx-pl-4 contrast-more:hx-border-current contrast-more:dark:hx-border-current hx-border-blue-200 hx-bg-blue-100 hx-text-blue-900 dark:hx-border-blue-200/30 dark:hx-bg-blue-900/30 dark:hx-text-blue-200"><div class="ltr:hx-pl-3 ltr:hx-pr-2 rtl:hx-pr-3 rtl:hx-pl-2"><div class="hx-select-none hx-text-xl" style="font-family:apple color emoji,segoe ui emoji,segoe ui symbol">ℹ️</div></div><div class="hx-w-full hx-min-w-0 hx-leading-7"><div class="hx-mt-6 hx-leading-7 first:hx-mt-0">GitHub-hosted runners provide a secure-by-default build environment. If self-hosted runners are required, run them on ephemeral VMs or containers, use separate runner groups for different security zones, and never use them for public repositories.</div></div></div><h4>PW.7: Review and/or analyze human-readable code to identify vulnerabilities<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=pw7-review-andor-analyze-human-readable-code-to-identify-vulnerabilities></span>
<a href=#pw7-review-andor-analyze-human-readable-code-to-identify-vulnerabilities class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h4><p><strong>Summary</strong>: Use manual and automated code review to find security issues.</p><p><strong>GitHub implementation scope</strong>: <em>GitHub-native</em></p><p><strong>Implementation details</strong>:</p><p>Use repository rulesets to enforce pull request requirements and code review processes, combined with automated and manual security reviews.</p><ol><li><p><strong>Repository rulesets for pull request requirements</strong>: Refer to the repository ruleset guidance in section PW.5 for code scanning enforcement. Additionally, configure repository rulesets to require pull requests before merging to default and protected branches:</p><ul><li><input checked disabled type=checkbox> Require pull request before merging<ul><li><input checked disabled type=checkbox> Required approving reviews: 1 (or more, depending on your risk tolerance)</li><li><input checked disabled type=checkbox> Dismiss stale pull request approvals when new commits are pushed</li><li><input checked disabled type=checkbox> Require review from Code Owners</li><li><input checked disabled type=checkbox> Require approval of the most recent reviewable push</li></ul></li></ul><p>Use <a href=https://docs.github.com/enterprise-cloud@latest/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/about-code-owners target=_blank rel=noopener>CODEOWNERS</a> files to identify and automatically tag required reviewers for security-sensitive code paths. This ensures that changes to critical areas (authentication, authorization, cryptography, payment processing, and the <code>.github</code> directory) are always reviewed by security champions or lead developers.</p></li><li><p><strong>Automated code scanning</strong>: Enable CodeQL or other SAST tools for automated security analysis. Require code scanning results in your repository rulesets to ensure all pull requests are scanned before merging.</p></li><li><p><strong>Code review suggestions</strong>: Use review comments to propose fixes directly in the pull request. Reviewers can suggest specific code changes that address concerns, and authors can apply them with a single click.</p></li><li><p><strong>Copilot code review</strong>: Automatically request <a href="https://docs.github.com/enterprise-cloud@latest/copilot/how-tos/use-copilot-agents/request-a-code-review/use-code-review?tool=webui" target=_blank rel=noopener>Copilot code review</a> with <a href="https://docs.github.com/enterprise-cloud@latest/copilot/how-tos/use-copilot-agents/request-a-code-review/use-code-review?tool=webui#customizing-copilots-reviews-with-custom-instructions" target=_blank rel=noopener>custom instructions</a> that emphasize security to automate initial security review.</p></li></ol><p><strong>Complete repository ruleset example for PW.7</strong>:</p><ul><li><p><input checked disabled type=checkbox> Require pull request before merging</p><ul><li><input checked disabled type=checkbox> Required approving reviews: 1</li><li><input checked disabled type=checkbox> Dismiss stale pull request approvals when new commits are pushed</li><li><input checked disabled type=checkbox> Require review from Code Owners</li><li><input checked disabled type=checkbox> Require approval of the most recent reviewable push</li></ul></li><li><p><input checked disabled type=checkbox> Require code scanning results</p><ul><li><input checked disabled type=checkbox> CodeQL - Security alerts: <code>High or higher</code>, Alerts: <code>Error</code></li></ul></li><li><p><input checked disabled type=checkbox> Automatically request Copilot code review</p></li></ul><h4>PW.8: Test executable code to identify vulnerabilities<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=pw8-test-executable-code-to-identify-vulnerabilities></span>
<a href=#pw8-test-executable-code-to-identify-vulnerabilities class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h4><p><strong>Summary</strong>: Use dynamic testing, fuzzing, and other runtime analysis to find security issues.</p><p><strong>GitHub implementation scope</strong>: <em>GitHub Integration</em></p><p><strong>Implementation details</strong>:</p><p>Integrate testing tools into GitHub Actions and upload SARIF results to GitHub code scanning.</p><ol><li><strong>DAST integration</strong>: Run dynamic application security testing on test deployments using Actions workflows</li><li><strong>Container scanning</strong>: Scan container images for vulnerabilities using third-party Actions (e.g., Trivy)</li><li><strong>Penetration testing</strong>: Document penetration testing findings in GitHub Issues</li></ol><p><strong>Example container scanning workflow</strong>:</p><div class="hextra-code-block hx-relative hx-mt-6 first:hx-mt-0 hx-group/code"><div><div class=highlight><pre tabindex=0 class=chroma><code class=language-yaml data-lang=yaml><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=nt>name</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>Container Security Scan</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w></span><span class=nt>on</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>  </span><span class=nt>push</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>    </span><span class=nt>branches</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=p>[</span><span class=l>main]</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>  </span><span class=nt>pull_request</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w></span><span class=nt>jobs</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>  </span><span class=nt>scan</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>    </span><span class=nt>runs-on</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>ubuntu-latest</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>    </span><span class=nt>steps</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>      </span>- <span class=nt>uses</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>actions/checkout@v5</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>      </span>- <span class=nt>name</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>Build container image</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>        </span><span class=nt>run</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>docker build -t myapp:${{ github.sha }} .</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>      </span>- <span class=nt>name</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>Run Trivy scanner</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>        </span><span class=nt>uses</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>aquasecurity/trivy-action@b6643a29fecd7f34b3597bc6acb0a98b03d33ff8</span><span class=w> </span><span class=c># v0.33.1</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>        </span><span class=nt>with</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>          </span><span class=nt>image-ref</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=s1>&#39;myapp:${{ github.sha }}&#39;</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>          </span><span class=nt>format</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=s1>&#39;sarif&#39;</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>          </span><span class=nt>output</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=s1>&#39;trivy-results.sarif&#39;</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>          </span><span class=nt>severity</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=s1>&#39;CRITICAL,HIGH&#39;</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>      </span>- <span class=nt>name</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>Upload Trivy results to GitHub Security</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>        </span><span class=nt>uses</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=l>github/codeql-action/upload-sarif@v3</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>        </span><span class=nt>with</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w>
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=w>          </span><span class=nt>sarif_file</span><span class=p>:</span><span class=w> </span><span class=s1>&#39;trivy-results.sarif&#39;</span></span></span></code></pre></div></div><div class="hextra-code-copy-btn-container hx-opacity-0 hx-transition group-hover/code:hx-opacity-100 hx-flex hx-gap-1 hx-absolute hx-m-[11px] hx-right-0 hx-top-0"><button class="hextra-code-copy-btn hx-group/copybtn hx-transition-all active:hx-opacity-50 hx-bg-primary-700/5 hx-border hx-border-black/5 hx-text-gray-600 hover:hx-text-gray-900 hx-rounded-md hx-p-1.5 dark:hx-bg-primary-300/10 dark:hx-border-white/10 dark:hx-text-gray-400 dark:hover:hx-text-gray-50" title="Copy code"><div class="copy-icon group-[.copied]/copybtn:hx-hidden hx-pointer-events-none hx-h-4 hx-w-4"></div><div class="success-icon hx-hidden group-[.copied]/copybtn:hx-block hx-pointer-events-none hx-h-4 hx-w-4"></div></button></div></div><h4>PW.9: Configure software to use secure settings by default<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=pw9-configure-software-to-use-secure-settings-by-default></span>
<a href=#pw9-configure-software-to-use-secure-settings-by-default class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h4><p><strong>Summary</strong>: Verify that released software has secure default configurations.</p><p><strong>GitHub implementation scope</strong>: <em>Out of scope</em></p><h3>Respond to Vulnerabilities (RV)<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=respond-to-vulnerabilities-rv></span>
<a href=#respond-to-vulnerabilities-rv class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><h4>RV.1: Identify and confirm vulnerabilities on an ongoing basis<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=rv1-identify-and-confirm-vulnerabilities-on-an-ongoing-basis></span>
<a href=#rv1-identify-and-confirm-vulnerabilities-on-an-ongoing-basis class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h4><p><strong>Summary</strong>: Continuously monitor for new vulnerabilities in software and dependencies.</p><p><strong>GitHub implementation scope</strong>: <em>GitHub-native</em></p><p><strong>Implementation details</strong>:</p><ol><li><p><strong>Security Overview</strong>: Use the multi-level <a href=https://docs.github.com/enterprise-cloud@latest/code-security/security-overview/about-security-overview target=_blank rel=noopener>Security Overview</a> dashboard to monitor vulnerability status:</p><ul><li><strong>Enterprise level</strong>: Aggregate security posture across all organizations</li><li><strong>Organization level</strong>: View alert trends, coverage gaps, and enablement status across repositories</li><li><strong>Repository level</strong>: Detailed alert status and remediation progress</li></ul></li><li><p><strong>Audit log streaming</strong>: Stream <a href=https://docs.github.com/enterprise-cloud@latest/admin/monitoring-activity-in-your-enterprise/reviewing-audit-logs-for-your-enterprise/streaming-the-audit-log-for-your-enterprise target=_blank rel=noopener>audit log events</a> to your SIEM for long-term retention, correlation with other security events, and compliance reporting.</p></li><li><p><strong>Webhooks for automated response</strong>: Configure <a href=https://docs.github.com/enterprise-cloud@latest/webhooks/about-webhooks target=_blank rel=noopener>webhooks</a> to automatically respond to security alert events.</p></li></ol><h4>RV.2: Assess, prioritize, and remediate vulnerabilities<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=rv2-assess-prioritize-and-remediate-vulnerabilities></span>
<a href=#rv2-assess-prioritize-and-remediate-vulnerabilities class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h4><p><strong>Summary</strong>: Triage and fix identified vulnerabilities based on risk.</p><p><strong>GitHub implementation scope</strong>: <em>GitHub-native</em></p><p><strong>Implementation details</strong>:</p><ol><li><strong>Security alerts</strong>: Review and triage alerts in the Security tab</li><li><strong>Dependabot security updates</strong>: Automatically generate PRs for dependency updates</li><li><strong>Repository custom properties</strong>: Use <a href=https://docs.github.com/enterprise-cloud@latest/organizations/managing-organization-settings/managing-custom-properties-for-repositories-in-your-organization target=_blank rel=noopener>custom properties</a> to classify repositories by business criticality, enabling risk-based prioritization of alerts</li><li><strong>Security campaigns</strong>: Use <a href=https://docs.github.com/enterprise-cloud@latest/code-security/securing-your-organization/tracking-security-work-across-your-organization/about-security-campaigns target=_blank rel=noopener>security campaigns</a> to prioritize and coordinate remediation of specific alert types across repositories</li><li><strong>Copilot Autofix</strong>: Use <a href=https://docs.github.com/enterprise-cloud@latest/code-security/code-scanning/managing-code-scanning-alerts/about-autofix-for-codeql-code-scanning target=_blank rel=noopener>Copilot Autofix</a> to automatically generate fix suggestions for vulnerabilities identified by CodeQL</li></ol><div class="hx-overflow-x-auto hx-mt-6 hx-flex hx-rounded-lg hx-border hx-py-2 ltr:hx-pr-4 rtl:hx-pl-4 contrast-more:hx-border-current contrast-more:dark:hx-border-current hx-border-blue-200 hx-bg-blue-100 hx-text-blue-900 dark:hx-border-blue-200/30 dark:hx-bg-blue-900/30 dark:hx-text-blue-200"><div class="ltr:hx-pl-3 ltr:hx-pr-2 rtl:hx-pr-3 rtl:hx-pl-2"><div class="hx-select-none hx-text-xl" style="font-family:apple color emoji,segoe ui emoji,segoe ui symbol">ℹ️</div></div><div class="hx-w-full hx-min-w-0 hx-leading-7"><div class="hx-mt-6 hx-leading-7 first:hx-mt-0">For more detailed guidance per alert type, refer to <a href=https://wellarchitected.github.com/library/application-security/recommendations/prioritizing-alerts/ target=_blank rel=noopener>Prioritizing Security Alert Remediation</a>.</div></div></div><h4>RV.3: Analyze vulnerabilities to identify their root causes<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=rv3-analyze-vulnerabilities-to-identify-their-root-causes></span>
<a href=#rv3-analyze-vulnerabilities-to-identify-their-root-causes class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h4><p><strong>Summary</strong>: Perform root cause analysis to prevent similar vulnerabilities.</p><p><strong>GitHub implementation scope</strong>: <em>GitHub-native</em></p><p><strong>Implementation details</strong>:</p><ol><li>Use GitHub Issues to document root cause analysis</li><li>Link related vulnerabilities using issue references</li><li>Create post-mortem templates for security incidents</li><li>Track systemic issues in Projects</li><li>Use Discussions for collaborative analysis</li></ol><p><strong>Root cause analysis template</strong>:</p><div class="hextra-code-block hx-relative hx-mt-6 first:hx-mt-0 hx-group/code"><div><div class=highlight><pre tabindex=0 class=chroma><code class=language-markdown data-lang=markdown><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu>## Vulnerability Root Cause Analysis
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu></span>
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gs>**Vulnerability ID**</span>: CVE-YYYY-XXXXX or GHSA-XXXXX
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gs>**Date Identified**</span>: YYYY-MM-DD
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gs>**Severity**</span>: Critical/High/Medium/Low
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl>
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu>### Summary
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu></span>Brief description of the vulnerability.
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl>
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu>### Root Cause
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu></span>What underlying issue allowed this vulnerability to exist?
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl>
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu>### Contributing Factors
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu></span><span class=k>-</span> Development process gaps
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=k>-</span> Tool/automation gaps
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=k>-</span> Training gaps
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=k>-</span> Design/architecture issues
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl>
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu>### Similar Issues
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu></span><span class=k>-</span> Link to related vulnerabilities
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=k>-</span> Common patterns identified
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl>
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu>### Preventive Actions
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu></span><span class=k>- [ ]</span> Process improvements
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=k>- [ ]</span> Tool/automation enhancements
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=k>- [ ]</span> Training updates
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=k>- [ ]</span> Architecture changes
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=k>- [ ]</span> Code scanning rules
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl>
</span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu>### Lessons Learned
</span></span></span><span class=line><span class=cl><span class=gu></span>Key takeaways to prevent recurrence.</span></span></code></pre></div></div><div class="hextra-code-copy-btn-container hx-opacity-0 hx-transition group-hover/code:hx-opacity-100 hx-flex hx-gap-1 hx-absolute hx-m-[11px] hx-right-0 hx-top-0"><button class="hextra-code-copy-btn hx-group/copybtn hx-transition-all active:hx-opacity-50 hx-bg-primary-700/5 hx-border hx-border-black/5 hx-text-gray-600 hover:hx-text-gray-900 hx-rounded-md hx-p-1.5 dark:hx-bg-primary-300/10 dark:hx-border-white/10 dark:hx-text-gray-400 dark:hover:hx-text-gray-50" title="Copy code"><div class="copy-icon group-[.copied]/copybtn:hx-hidden hx-pointer-events-none hx-h-4 hx-w-4"></div><div class="success-icon hx-hidden group-[.copied]/copybtn:hx-block hx-pointer-events-none hx-h-4 hx-w-4"></div></button></div></div><h2>Additional solution detail and trade-offs to consider<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=additional-solution-detail-and-trade-offs-to-consider></span>
<a href=#additional-solution-detail-and-trade-offs-to-consider class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h2><h3>Implementation approach<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=implementation-approach></span>
<a href=#implementation-approach class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p><strong>Phased rollout</strong>: Implementing NIST SSDF compliance across an organization is a significant undertaking. Consider a phased approach:</p><ol><li><strong>Phase 1 - Foundation</strong>: Enable core security features (SSO, MFA, audit logging)</li><li><strong>Phase 2 - Detection</strong>: Activate scanning capabilities (code scanning, secret scanning, Dependabot)</li><li><strong>Phase 3 - Enforcement</strong>: Implement rulesets, required workflows, and automated policy enforcement</li><li><strong>Phase 4 - Optimization</strong>: Fine-tune configurations, develop custom queries, and streamline processes</li></ol><p><strong>Pilot programs</strong>: Start with a subset of critical repositories (5-10 repositories) or teams to validate configurations and workflows before organization-wide rollout. Successful pilots typically run 4-6 weeks and identify ~80% of edge cases and configuration issues before broader deployment.</p><p><strong>Lessons learned from field deployments</strong>:</p><ul><li>Communicate early and often with development teams about upcoming changes</li><li>Provide self-service documentation and troubleshooting guides</li><li>Establish clear escalation paths for security exceptions</li><li>Celebrate quick wins and security improvements to build momentum</li></ul><h3>GitHub Enterprise Cloud vs. Server<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=github-enterprise-cloud-vs-server></span>
<a href=#github-enterprise-cloud-vs-server class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p><strong>GitHub Enterprise Cloud</strong> provides the most comprehensive feature set for SSDF compliance.</p><p><strong>GitHub Enterprise Server</strong> supports most SSDF practices but may lag behind Cloud in feature availability. Organizations with air-gapped or on-premises requirements should evaluate feature parity for their GHES version.</p><p><strong>GitHub Enterprise Cloud with Enterprise Managed Users (EMU)</strong> provides enhanced security and governance for organizations requiring strict control over identity and access management.</p><h3>Tool integration trade-offs<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=tool-integration-trade-offs></span>
<a href=#tool-integration-trade-offs class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p><strong>GitHub-native vs. third-party tools</strong>: GitHub Advanced Security provides comprehensive scanning capabilities that integrate seamlessly with the platform. However, organizations may need to supplement with specialized tools for:</p><ul><li>Interactive application security testing (IAST)</li><li>Runtime application self-protection (RASP)</li><li>Dynamic application security testing (DAST)</li><li>Container scanning</li><li>Threat modeling</li></ul><p><strong>Integration approach</strong>: When integrating external tools:</p><ul><li>Use GitHub Actions for orchestration</li><li>Upload code scanning results to GitHub Security tab using SARIF format</li><li>Implement tools as required status checks</li></ul><h3>Automation boundaries<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=automation-boundaries></span>
<a href=#automation-boundaries class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p><strong>What to automate</strong>: Security scanning, dependency updates, policy enforcement, artifact generation, and basic triage should be fully automated to ensure consistency and reduce human error.</p><p><strong>What requires human judgment</strong>: Security design reviews, risk acceptance decisions, root cause analysis, and security exceptions require human expertise and should not be fully automated.</p><h3>Audit and compliance considerations<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=audit-and-compliance-considerations></span>
<a href=#audit-and-compliance-considerations class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p><strong>Evidence generation</strong>: GitHub provides extensive audit trails through:</p><ul><li>Audit log API and streaming</li><li>Workflow run histories and artifacts</li><li>Security alert histories</li><li>Git commit history</li><li>Release artifacts and attestations</li></ul><p><strong>Retention requirements</strong>: Configure audit log streaming to a SIEM or log management system for long-term retention that exceeds GitHub&rsquo;s default retention periods.</p><p><strong>Third-party audits</strong>: GitHub&rsquo;s audit capabilities and artifact generation features provide evidence for SOC 2, FedRAMP, and other compliance audits. Ensure your configuration generates the specific artifacts required by your auditors.</p><h3>Performance and scalability<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=performance-and-scalability></span>
<a href=#performance-and-scalability class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p><strong>Code scanning impact</strong>: CodeQL and other SAST tools can increase CI/CD pipeline duration. Optimize by:</p><ul><li>Parallelizing scans across languages</li><li>Caching build dependencies</li><li>Using larger runners for resource-intensive scans</li></ul><p><strong>Large organization scaling</strong>: Organizations with thousands of repositories should:</p><ul><li>Use organization-level rulesets instead of per-repository configuration</li><li>Leverage security configurations for consistent settings across repositories</li><li>Use required workflows to enforce security scans</li><li>Implement repository custom properties for targeted policy application</li></ul><h3>Common implementation challenges<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=common-implementation-challenges></span>
<a href=#common-implementation-challenges class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p><strong>Developer experience</strong>: Security controls can be perceived as friction. Address this by:</p><ul><li>Providing clear documentation and training</li><li>Automating fix suggestions (Copilot Autofix, Dependabot PRs)</li><li>Establishing reasonable response SLAs</li><li>Creating fast feedback loops</li><li>Offering self-service exception processes for legitimate use cases</li></ul><p><strong>False positive management</strong>: Scanning tools can generate false positives. Mitigate by:</p><ul><li>Documenting alert dismissal rationales</li><li>Regularly reviewing dismissed alert reasons</li></ul><p><strong>Legacy code challenges</strong>: Existing codebases may have numerous security findings. Approach with:</p><ul><li>Risk-based prioritization focusing on high-severity issues</li><li>Gradual remediation tied to normal development work</li><li>Clear separation of new vs. legacy code expectations</li><li>Baseline establishment with improvement targets</li></ul><h3>Cost considerations<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=cost-considerations></span>
<a href=#cost-considerations class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p><strong>Actions minutes</strong>: Security scanning consumes Actions minutes. Manage costs through:</p><ul><li>Using larger runners for faster execution</li><li>Optimizing scan frequency</li><li>Leveraging caching</li></ul><p><strong>External tool costs</strong>: Third-party integrations may have separate licensing costs. Evaluate whether GitHub-native features can meet requirements before adding external tools.</p><h2>Seeking further assistance<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=seeking-further-assistance></span>
<a href=#seeking-further-assistance class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h2><h3>GitHub Support<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=github-support></span>
<a href=#github-support class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p>Visit the <a href=https://support.github.com/ target=_blank rel=noopener>GitHub Support Portal</a> for a comprehensive collection of articles, tutorials, and guides on using GitHub features and services.</p><p>Can&rsquo;t find what you&rsquo;re looking for? You can contact GitHub Support by <a href=https://support.github.com/contact target=_blank rel=noopener>opening a ticket</a>.</p><h3>GitHub Expert Services<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=github-expert-services></span>
<a href=#github-expert-services class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p>GitHub&rsquo;s <a href=https://github.com/services target=_blank rel=noopener>Expert Services Team</a> is here to help you architect, implement, and optimize a solution that meets your unique needs. <a href=https://github.com/services#services-contact target=_blank rel=noopener>Contact us</a> to learn more about how we can help you.</p><h3>GitHub Partners<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=github-partners></span>
<a href=#github-partners class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p>GitHub partners with the world&rsquo;s leading technology and service providers to help our customers achieve their end-to-end business objectives. Find a GitHub Partner that can help you with your specific needs <a href=https://portal.github.partners/#/page/directory target=_blank rel=noopener>here</a>.</p><h3>GitHub Community<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=github-community></span>
<a href=#github-community class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p>Join the <a href="https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions?discussions_q=label%3A%22GitHub+Well-Architected%22" target=_blank rel=noopener>GitHub Community Forum</a> to ask questions, share knowledge, and connect with other GitHub users. It’s a great place to get advice and solutions from experienced users.</p><h2>Related links<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=related-links></span>
<a href=#related-links class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h2><h3>GitHub Documentation<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=github-documentation></span>
<a href=#github-documentation class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><p>For more details about GitHub&rsquo;s features and services, check out <a href=https://docs.github.com/ target=_blank rel=noopener>GitHub Documentation</a>.</p><h3>External resources<span class="hx-absolute -hx-mt-20" id=external-resources></span>
<a href=#external-resources class=subheading-anchor aria-label="Permalink for this section"></a></h3><ul><li><a href=https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/218/final target=_blank rel=noopener>NIST SP 800-218: Secure Software Development Framework</a></li><li><a href=https://slsa.dev/ target=_blank rel=noopener>Supply-chain Levels for Software Artifacts (SLSA)</a></li></ul></div><div class="hx-mt-12 hx-mb-8 hx-block hx-text-xs hx-text-gray-500 ltr:hx-text-right rtl:hx-text-left dark:hx-text-gray-400">Last updated on <time datetime=2026-01-23T12:26:27.000Z>January 23, 2026</time></div><div class="hx-mb-8 hx-flex hx-items-center hx-border-t hx-pt-8 dark:hx-border-neutral-800 contrast-more:hx-border-neutral-400 dark:contrast-more:hx-border-neutral-400 print:hx-hidden"><a href=/library/scenarios/monorepos/ title=Monorepos class="hx-flex hx-max-w-[50%] hx-items-center hx-gap-1 hx-py-4 hx-text-base hx-font-medium hx-text-gray-600 hx-transition-colors [word-break:break-word] hover:hx-text-primary-600 dark:hx-text-gray-300 md:hx-text-lg ltr:hx-pr-4 rtl:hx-pl-4"><svg class="hx-inline hx-h-5 hx-shrink-0 ltr:hx-rotate-180" fill="none" viewBox="0 0 24 24" stroke-width="2" stroke="currentColor" aria-hidden="true"><path stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" d="M9 5l7 7-7 7"/></svg>Monorepos</a></div></main></article></div><footer class="hextra-footer hx-bg-gray-100 hx-pb-[env(safe-area-inset-bottom)] dark:hx-bg-neutral-900 print:hx-bg-transparent"><div class="hx-max-w-screen-xl hx-mx-auto hx-flex hx-justify-center hx-py-12 hx-pl-[max(env(safe-area-inset-left),1.5rem)] hx-pr-[max(env(safe-area-inset-right),1.5rem)] hx-text-gray-600 dark:hx-text-gray-400 md:hx-justify-start"><div class="hx-flex hx-w-full hx-flex-col hx-items-center sm:hx-items-start hx-gap-y-2"><div class="hx-flex hx-flex-wrap hx-gap-1 hx-items-center hx-justify-center hx-w-full hx-mt-1 hx-text-xs"><a href=https://github.com/about style=color:#4493fb;margin-right:1rem;margin-left:1rem>About</a>
